<margin>All growths derive their beginning from the upper stars.</margin>
I recently discovered, however, that all internal mountain-growths <note>minerals and ores</note> were poured out from the upper mountain-stars, and received their beginning from a watery vapor, smoke, or water-mist. This was further nourished over a long time by the stars and made into a tangible form by the elements. Through this process, such vapor was dried out so that the wateriness lost its dominion, and the fire—following the water and with the help of the air—gained the upper hand. Thus, at one time, fire came from water, and from fire and air came earth. These elements are still found inseparably in every physical thing in the world before their separation.

# Of Antimony

Whether the Stone of Fire <note>Latin: Stein Ignis; a reference to a specific alchemical preparation of antimony believed to possess great heat and transformative power</note> exists, also concerning its mountain-matter <note>The raw ore as it is found in the earth</note>, and whether a Stone can be made without matter or not. Likewise, concerning the final distinction of the Stones, and how many kinds of them are found; and lastly, concerning their use.

For this my undertaking, may the Spirit of Heaven grant me that I may report uprightly, yet only so much as is permitted to me: so I will hope for eternal absolution from my highest Father Confessor <note>A religious metaphor for God, common in the writings of Basilius Valentinus</note>, who infinitely possesses the Throne of Grace from eternity, and will give testimony of all things when the final sentence and the last judgment over all people of the world shall follow, without any protestation or appeal.

<margin>The matter of the true Tincture of Antimony</margin>
Initially and before all things, you should know and be informed of this: that the right, true Tincture of Antimony <note>A "Tincture" in alchemy refers to a concentrated medicinal or transformative essence</note>, which is a human and metallic cure <note>Latin: Cura; implying it heals both diseased bodies and "sick" or base metals</note>, is not made from the raw, melted antimony <note>German: Spießglase; the common name for stibnite or crude antimony</note> as the same is encountered and found at the grocers and in the pharmacies.

# The Triumphal Chariot

found; rather, it is drawn out from the mineral or from the ore, just as it is yielded from the mountains, and has previously been made into a glass. However, how such an <term>extraction<note>original: "Extractio"</note></term> proceeds is the entire masterpiece and the whole of the art: whoever hits upon this correctly can become rich in his health and in his goods.

<margin>

Man is subject to many strange and dangerous diseases, which indeed completely weaken and take away the natural strengths of some. Now, these same diseases can be countered through remedies in such a way that he—the human being—is rightly helped and returns to his former health.

<margin>Side-works on Love.</margin>
Love surpasses all other infirmities and diseases, for it can in no way be helped except through a remedy of its own kind, which is the desire of both natures; this is only extinguished through the fulfilled will of the ignited, conceived desire. Indeed, even in this present hour, many must testify to what love does, for it attacks not only the young, but also the old, who burn entirely, rave, and on account of love rage so in their old age that they become complete and utter fools because of it.

# Triumphal Chariot

<margin>
Practice
of the
Fire
Stone.
</margin>

In the name of the Most High, take one part of Antimony ore <note>original: "Spießglas-Erz"</note> that has grown toward the sunrise, and one part of saltpeter that is good and pure. Grind it all into a fine powder and mix it together. Then place it in a moderate fire; burn it or calcine it together according to the manual technique, which is the masterpiece. There will remain for you a material inclined toward a brown color; make this into a glass. Grind this same glass small once again, and extract the deep red tincture from it with a pure, good, and sharp distilled vinegar made from its own mineral. Draw off the vinegar through distillation in a <term>water bath</term> <note>original: "Balneo Mariæ," a vessel of water used to heat another container gently</note>. There will remain (yyy) a powder behind; extract this once more with <term>spirit of wine</term> <note>original: "Spiritu Vini," highly rectified alcohol</note> brought to its highest strength. The <term>dregs</term> <note>original: "Feces," the solid residue remaining after extraction</note> remain be-
<meta>catchword: hind-</meta>

---

# Of Antimony

behind, and you will have an exceedingly beautiful red extraction, sweet and lovely, which is highly useful to be employed in medicine.

This is now the pure *Sulfur of Antimony* <note>original: "Sulphur Antimonii"; in alchemy, "sulfur" refers to the combustible, oily, or "soul" component of a substance</note>, separated to the highest degree. If you have two pounds of this extraction, take eight ounces of *Salt of Antimony* <note>original: "Salis Antimonii"</note>, as I have taught you to make, pour this extraction over it, and circulate it well together for at least one month, well-sealed; thus the salt will have united itself with the extraction of the *Sulfur*. If it has deposited any dregs <note>original: "Fæces"</note>, then separate them from it, and first distill the *Spirit of Wine* <note>original: "Spiritum Vini"; concentrated alcohol</note> from it in the *Bath of Mary* <note>original: "Balneo Mariae"; a gentle water bath or double boiler</note>. The powder that remains for the artist should be driven with a strong fire; then, with great and unbelievable wonder of many colors, a lovely sweet *Oil* <note>original: "Oleum"</note> will come over, entirely transparent and red in its resolution. *Rectify* <note>distill again to purify</note> this oil once more in the *Bath of Mary* so that the fourth part of it comes over; then it is prepared rightly.

# The Triumphal Chariot

<note>The title refers to "The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony," a famous alchemical text attributed to Basil Valentine.</note>

Take the <term>mercury of antimony</term><note>original: "mercurium des Spießglases"; antimony was known as "Spießglas" in early modern German</note> just as I have taught you to prepare it, and pour upon it a red **Oil of Vitriol** <note>sulfuric acid</note> made from iron and rectified to the highest degree. Distill off the <term>phlegm</term><note>the watery, non-medicinal byproduct of distillation</note> from the mercury in a sand bath; thus the seeker of the Art shall obtain a truly magnificent and beautiful <term>precipitate</term><note>a solid substance separated from a solution</note>, the likes of which has never been seen, possessing a lovely color. It proves its worth in the healing of many long-standing open wounds and diseases, for it powerfully dries out the incidental moisture from which <term>martial diseases</term><note>ailments associated with the planet Mars and the metal iron, often involving inflammation or blood</note> arise. To this end, the spirit of the oil—which remains behind with the mercury and unites with it—is a mighty aid.

<meta>The catchword "Die" indicates the start of the next section.</meta>

---

Mercury, which has so often left you with an ambiguous understanding, must also be rightly understood here; for if you have not taken the true **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Mercurium Philosophorum"; a sophisticated alchemical substance, not common quicksilver</note>, you will accomplish nothing. Whoever it may be that has sincerely revealed this to you, let him be a <term>Pylades</term> to you, and you an <term>Orestes</term> to him <note>famous figures from Greek mythology representing the highest form of loyal friendship</note>; and nothing in my life shall be more pleasant to me than to serve as the third man and companion in friendship to you both.

My dear disciple, apostle, or artist of the mystery <note>The author addresses the reader as an initiate into the "Great Work" of alchemy</note>: I will now speak in the manner and style of the Prophets, and say thus: When you have brought your studies of *Philosophy* <note>original: "Philosophiæ"; here meaning natural philosophy or alchemy</note> to such a conclusion, alongside the completed labor of *Antimony* <note>original: "Antimonii"</note> as I have now and herewith prescribed for you, then you possess a medicine for both humans and metals. It is sweet and lovely to use without any danger; it is also penetrating, and causes absolutely no *bowel movements* <note>original: "Sedes"; the author emphasizes that unlike crude antimony, this refined medicine does not act as a violent purgative</note>, but rather brings about improvement and drives away all evil. For this, you will be unable to find any thank-offering sufficient to repay such a benefit.

# of Antimony <note>original: "Antimonii"</note>

> Oh **GOD!** I speak now with a troubled spirit and as a spiritual man; for I do not know whether I act rightly or wrongly, whether I do too much (bbbb) or too little in these matters. I leave it to the judgment of others, and to you, disciples and followers: test yourselves even as I have done. If you hit the mark, then thank **GOD** and me as your *teacher* <note>original: "Præceptori"</note> and schoolmaster. But if you wander onto erring paths and turn aside so that you do not find the true way, then that remains your own burden; keep the consequences for yourselves, pass judgment upon yourselves, and leave me in peace, for I am not guilty of your wandering.

I have now spoken enough, written enough, and also taught enough—so bright and clear, indeed so plain, that one could not do it better with writings.

<sig>X 2</sig>
<meta>from:</meta>

<margin>(bbbb) The author thinks he has written too much; if you are of the same opinion, you will find joy in his regret. However, it is a wonder that no one has brought the slightest light to this *philosophy* <note>meaning alchemy and natural secrets</note> without it immediately thereafter causing him remorse.</margin>

<summary>The author expresses a profound spiritual anxiety over revealing alchemical secrets, warning his students that they alone are responsible for any failures or errors in their practice. He asserts that his instructions on Antimony are as clear as written language allows.</summary>

# The Triumphal Chariot

Now I will write no more concerning <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimonio"; a metallic element used in alchemy, believed by the author to hold the key to universal medicine</note>, for I consider that I have done enough for these matters on my own part. If another comes after me, let him also do his part, so that the <term>Mysteries</term> <note>original: "Mysteria"; hidden spiritual or natural truths</note> may be revealed before the end of the world—as God has permitted—to His praise and for the preservation of health.

With this, I shall remain silent and return for a time to my spiritual Order, until I take up my <term>Philosophy</term> <note>original: "Philosophiam"; here referring to alchemical study and the pursuit of natural wisdom</note> once more to write further of other things. As will now follow, it is my desired intention, by the permission of God, the Prince of Heaven, to provide a further report and description—as I have long promised—concerning <term>Sulphur</term> and <term>Magnets</term>, their origin, power, operation, and virtue.

May the eternal Prince of Heaven grant us temporal health, alongside and above all, eternal health, for comfort and refreshment in that life to come—

<summary>The author concludes his treatise on Antimony, announcing his return to monastic life before beginning a promised work on the alchemical properties of Sulphur and Magnets.</summary>
<keywords>Antimony, Alchemy, Sulphur, Magnets, Philosophy, Divine Providence, Health</keywords>

awakening of our souls, and of the everlasting hall of joy. Amen.

Thus I hereby conclude my treatise on Antimony <note>original: "Spießglas," a historical term for the mineral stibnite</note>, and what I have further written concerning the **red oil** of Antimony, which was made from the highest purified sulfur; as well as concerning the **spirit** <note>a volatile essence or distillate</note> which was prepared from its salt.
